<meta>This page continues the 1913 Annual Report of the Irish Texts Society, providing a catalog of upcoming volumes and scholarly editions currently in preparation by various editors.</meta>

The following are among the volumes that have been offered to the Society, several of which are in various stages of preparation:

### POETRY.

(1) The third and final volume of the Poems of David O’Bruadair <note>David O’Bruadair (c. 1625–1698) was a significant Irish poet who witnessed the downfall of the old Gaelic order</note>, edited by Reverend J. MacErlean, S.J.

(2) Bardic Poems of Tadhg Dall O’Higgin <note>Tadhg Dall Ó hUiginn (1550–1591) was one of the most famous professional poets of the late 16th century</note>, edited by Miss Eleanor Knott.

(3) Bardic Poems relating to the O’Neills of Clandeboy <note>The O’Neills of Clandeboy were a powerful branch of the O'Neill dynasty in northeast Ulster</note>, edited by Tadhg O’Donoghue, B.A.

(4) Bardic Poems relating to the O’Reilly and Maguire families, edited by Dr. E. Quiggin, F.G.C.C., Cambridge.

(5) The Poem-book of Hugh mac Shane O’Byrne <note>Hugh mac Shane O'Byrne (died 1579) was a Chieftain of the O'Byrnes of Wicklow known for his resistance to Tudor expansion</note>, edited by J. H. Lloyd.

(6) The Contention of the Bards <note>original: Iomarbhágh na bhFileadh; a famous early 17th-century poetic dispute over the relative merits of the northern and southern halves of Ireland</note>, edited by Professor T. O’Nowlan.

### ROMANCE AND FOLK LORE

(7) Irish Folk Tales, edited by Professor Douglas Hyde, LL.D. <note>Douglas Hyde was a leading figure in the Gaelic Revival and later the first President of Ireland</note>

(8) A new version of the <term>Agallamh na Senorach</term> <note>original: Agallamh na Senorach; translated as "The Colloquy of the Ancients," a classic Middle Irish narrative featuring dialogues between Saint Patrick and the heroes of the Fenian cycle</note>, edited by Professor Douglas Hyde, LL.D.

(9) A late Meath romance, <term>Teagbhalaibh Dubh Mac Deaghla</term> <note>original: Teagbhalaibh Dubh Mac Deaghla; translated as "The Adventures of Black Mac Deaghla"</note>, edited by Joseph H. Lloyd.

### TRANSLATIONS.

(10) An Astronomical Tract, edited by Miss Maura Power, M.A.

(11) The Irish version of Statius <note>This refers to the "Togail na Tebe" (The Destruction of Thebes), an Irish adaptation of the Latin epic by the Roman poet Statius</note>, edited by Reverend Professor George Calder, B.D.

### SAINTS’ LIVES.

(12) Lives of Saint Declan <note>St. Declan of Ardmore was a pre-Patrician saint in Munster</note> and Saint Mochuda <note>St. Mochuda (also known as St. Carthage) was the 7th-century founder of the monastery at Lismore</note>, edited by Reverend P. Power.

### INDEXES

->(13) The Indexes and Genealogies for Keating’s *History of Ireland* <note>original: *Foras Feasa ar Éirinn*; a fundamental 17th-century history of Ireland written by Geoffrey Keating (Seathrún Céitinn)</note>, edited by Reverend P. S. Dinneen, M.A.<-

The Council is pleased to note that the second volume of Keating’s *History of Ireland* has been included in the degree program of the National University of Ireland for the current year.

### DICTIONARIES

Dictionary sales have been steady; over 200 copies of the larger dictionary and about 800 copies of the smaller dictionary have been sold during the year. <note>The "larger dictionary" refers to Dinneen's famous Irish-English dictionary first published in 1904</note>

The total number of members now stands at 663, with twenty-nine new members having joined the Society during the year:

The adoption of the Report was proposed by Mr. R. Flower, seconded by Mr. T. W. Rolleston, supported by Mr. J. Bartholomew, and passed.

Mr. S. Boyle, Honorary Treasurer, then presented the Annual Financial Report and Balance Sheet.

<summary>This page contains the financial report of the Irish Texts Society for 1913, showing healthy balances for both its general publication fund and its specialized dictionary fund. --- Page 262 ---
<meta>This page continues the financial and progress report of the Irish Texts Society for the 1912–1913 period, focusing on publication status and donations.</meta>

In presenting this report, the Honorable Treasurer, Mr. Boyle, continued the interesting review <note>original: "retrospect"</note> of the Society’s work that he began at last year’s meeting. He showed that the Society has achieved a success exceeding even the most optimistic <note>original: "sanguine"</note> hopes of its original founders. The work of the past year has placed the Society in an even more satisfactory position. 

Throughout the Society’s existence, it has been a matter of serious concern to the Council and a cause of misunderstanding for members worldwide that the learned editors of the volumes have occasionally found it impossible to finish their work on time. To issue each volume within its designated year would have required haste and resulted in inaccuracies, which would be inconsistent with the high standard of excellence the Council and editors aim to maintain. Having issued two volumes this year, the Society is now practically up to date with its publications. We are only behind for the year 1912, and we hope that the volume for that year will be issued soon. 

Regarding the actual cost of production (printing, binding, etc.) for the volumes, Mr. Boyle provided some interesting figures. The production cost for the thirteen volumes, separate from editorial and administrative expenses, amounted to nearly £2,000. It is gratifying to report that this entire expenditure—initially covered by the publishers—has now been repaid, except for a sum of about £150 still owed for the last three volumes issued. We hope current members will help recruit a large number of new members to subscribe for back volumes still in stock. This would soon pay off the small debt to the publishers. After that, the sale of back volumes will provide a substantial and permanent annual income for the Society. This will enable the Council to undertake much important work that our current limited resources cannot support.

It will be noted that receipts listed under "donations," which amounted to only £28 in 1911–12, have increased to £150 in 1912–13.

This pleasing increase is chiefly due to two generous donations. One came from a member in Nice who sent the Honorable Treasurer a magnificent <note>original: "munificent"</note> gift of £125. The other was a gift from Lisbon from a noble <note>original: "princely"</note> representative of the great O’Neill family of Ulster. Additionally, many members, when sending their annual subscriptions, chose to pay 10 shillings—namely <note>original: "viz."</note>, the 7s. 6d. subscription fee and...

<summary>The Irish Texts Society reports that it is nearly caught up on its publication schedule and has successfully repaid most of its production debts. The Society also notes a significant increase in donations, including major gifts from members in Nice and Lisbon.</summary>
<keywords>Irish Texts Society, Mr. Boyle, book production costs, O'Neill family, Ulster, publication backlog, financial report</keywords>

--- Page 263 ---
<meta>This page concludes the annual meeting report for the Irish Texts Society, detailing administrative changes, the election of officers, and the Society's long-term vision for Gaelic literature.</meta>

...the two-shilling and six-pence donation <note>original: "2s. 6d. donation"; this was a modest sum, roughly equivalent to one-eighth of a pound sterling at the time.</note>, a kind and thoughtful means of furthering the interests of the Irish Texts Society in its great aim of rescuing from the obscurity <note>original: "oblivion"</note> of libraries the priceless treasures of Gaelic literature still left to us, to serve as the foundations and models for a great Gaelic literature of the future.

It was gratifying to have present at the annual meeting a respected representative of the Scottish Gaels, Mr. J. Bartholomew of Edinburgh.

The formal acceptance <note>original: "adoption"</note> of the Report was proposed by Mr. J. Buckley, seconded by Mr. Frank MacDonagh, and carried.

A warm vote of thanks to the auditors for their kind services in auditing the accounts for the year was proposed by Mr. Rolleston, seconded by Mr. Boyle, and carried unanimously.

Miss Hull proposed, and Mr. Rolleston seconded, the following addition to Rule IV:

> “. . . . . . to whom the Executive Council may add by co-option <note>original: "co-option"; the process by which a committee or council elects its own additional members rather than having them chosen by the general membership.</note> not more than two members, who shall retire annually.”

After discussion, the new addition to the Rule was carried unanimously.

The retiring members of Council—Mr. A. W. K. Miller, Head of Printed Books at the British Museum; Mr. Ernest Rhys; and Mr. J. Buckley, M.R.I.A. <note>original: "M.R.I.A."; Member of the Royal Irish Academy.</note>—were re-elected.

The officers of the Society were re-elected as follows:
* **Professor Douglas Hyde, LL.D.** <note>original: "LL.D."; Doctor of Laws. Hyde was a key figure in the Gaelic Revival and later the first President of Ireland.</note> (President)
* **Miss Eleanor Hull** (Honorary Secretary)
* **Mr. Samuel Boyle** (Honorary Treasurer)

Mr. E. W. Lynam and Mr. C. R. Cooke-Taylor were re-elected as auditors.

The meeting then terminated.

<summary>This page records the conclusion of an annual meeting of the Irish Texts Society, including the re-election of prominent figures like Douglas Hyde and the amendment of Society rules regarding committee appointments.</summary>
<keywords>Irish Texts Society, Gaelic literature, Douglas Hyde, Eleanor Hull, Rule IV, co-option, British Museum, Royal Irish Academy, Scottish Gaels</keywords>

--- Page 264 ---
<meta>This page outlines the official General Rules of the Irish Texts Society, defining its purpose, leadership structure, and the administrative powers of its Executive Council and Consultative Committee.</meta>

# GENERAL RULES.

### OBJECTS.

1. The Society is established for the purpose of promoting the publication of texts written in the Irish language. These publications are to be accompanied by introductions, English translations, glossaries, and notes, as the Society sees fit.

### CONSTITUTION.

2. The Society shall consist of a President, Vice-Presidents, an Executive Council, a Consultative Committee, and Ordinary Members.

### OFFICERS.

3. The Officers of the Society shall be the President, the Honorary Secretary, and the Honorary Treasurer.

### EXECUTIVE COUNCIL.

4. The entire management of the Society shall be entrusted to the Executive Council. This council consists of the Officers of the Society and no more than ten other members. Additionally, the Executive Council may add up to two members through co-option <note>original: "Co-option"; a process where the existing committee elects its own additional members rather than having them chosen by the general membership.</note>, and these members must retire annually.

5. All property of the Society shall be held by the Executive Council and shall be managed or used as the Council directs by a two-thirds majority vote.

6. Three members of the Executive Council shall retire each year by rotation at the Annual General Meeting, though they shall be eligible for re-election. The members who retire will be selected based on how long they have served (seniority) or, in the case of equal tenure, by drawing lots. The Council has the power to appoint members to fill any unexpected vacancies <note>original: "casual vacancies"</note> that occur during the year. Any member of the Council who is absent from five consecutive regular meetings to which they have been officially invited <note>original: "duly summoned"</note> will be considered to have vacated their seat on the Council.

### CONSULTATIVE COMMITTEE.

7. The Consultative Committee, or its individual members, shall provide advice when asked by the Executive Council on matters regarding the Society’s publications. However, they shall not be responsible for managing the Society’s business affairs.

### MEMBERS.

8. Members may be elected either at the Annual General Meeting or, from time to time, by the Executive Council.

<summary>This page details the governing rules of the Irish Texts Society, including its mission to publish Irish literature and the administrative structure of its leadership councils.</summary>
<keywords>Irish Texts Society, bylaws, Executive Council, Consultative Committee, organizational structure, Irish language preservation</keywords>

--- Page 265 ---
<meta>This page continues the "General Rules" of the Irish Texts Society, focusing on membership fees, the funding of editors, and the scheduling of annual meetings and rule changes.</meta>

### SUBSCRIPTION.

9\. The annual subscription for each Member of the Society shall be 7 shillings and 6 pence <note>7/6 refers to 7 shillings and 6 pence in pre-decimal British currency.</note> per year <note>original: "per annum"</note> (for American subscribers, two dollars). This entitles the Member to one copy (shipped for free) of the volume or volumes published by the Society for that year and grants them the right to vote on all questions brought before the General Meetings of the Society.

10\. Subscriptions shall be payable in advance on January 1st of each year.

11\. Members whose subscriptions for the year have not been paid are not entitled to receive any volume published by the Society for that year. Any Member who has not paid for the current year, but who receives and keeps any publication for that year, shall be held responsible for paying the full retail price of that publication.

12\. The publications of the Society shall not be sold to people other than Members, except at a higher <note>original: "advanced"</note> price.

13\. Only Members whose subscriptions for the current year have been paid shall have the right to vote at the General Meetings of the Society.

14\. Members wishing to resign must give written notice to the Honorary Secretary before the end of the year. If they do not, they will be responsible for their subscription for the following year.

### EDITORIAL FUND.

15\. A fund shall be established to pay <note>original: "remuneration"</note> Editors for their work in preparing texts for publication. All subscriptions and donations to this fund shall be entirely voluntary and shall not be used for any other purposes of the Society.

### ANNUAL GENERAL MEETING.

16\. A General Meeting shall be held each year in the month of April, or as soon afterward as the Executive Council decides. At this meeting, the Council shall submit their Report and the Society's accounts for the preceding year. Any open positions <note>original: "seats to be vacated"</note> on the Council shall be filled, and the standard business of a General Meeting will be conducted.

### AUDIT.

17\. The accounts of the Society shall be audited <note>formally inspected for accuracy</note> each year by auditors appointed at the previous General Meeting.

### CHANGES IN THESE RULES.

18\. Along with the notice calling the General Meeting, the Executive Council shall give notice of any changes they propose to these Rules. Ordinary Members proposing any change in the Rules must give written notice to the Honorary Secretary seven full days <note>original: "seven clear days"; a legal term meaning seven complete days must pass between the notice and the meeting.</note> before the date of the Annual General Meeting.

<summary>This page details the financial and administrative regulations of the Irish Texts Society, including membership dues, the voluntary fund for editors, and the procedures for annual meetings and auditing accounts.</summary>
<keywords>Irish Texts Society, subscription fees, membership rights, Editorial Fund, Annual General Meeting, auditing, corporate governance, rule changes</keywords>

# LIST OF IRISH TEXTS SOCIETY PUBLICATIONS

## CURRENT AND PREVIOUSLY ISSUED VOLUMES.

---

->(*Issued 1899. Out of print.*)<-

1. *The Lad of the Ferrule* <note>original Irish: "Giolla an Fiuġa"</note>.

->*Adventures of the Children of the King of Norway* <note>original Irish: "Eaċtra Cloinne Ríġ na h-Ioruaiḋe"</note>.<-

->(Texts from the 16th and 17th centuries.)<-

->Edited by DOUGLAS HYDE, LL.D. <note>Hyde was a scholar who later became the first President of Ireland.</note><-

---

->(*Issued 1899. Out of print.*)<-

2. *The Feast of Bricriu* <note>original Irish: "Fleḋ Bricrend"</note>.

->(From the *Book of the Dun Cow* <note>original Irish: "Leabhar na h-Uidhre," the oldest surviving manuscript in Irish</note>, with the conclusion from<-
->Gaelic Manuscript 40 in the Advocates’ Library, and variations from the British Museum<-
->Egerton 93 manuscript; Trinity College Dublin H. 3. 17; and Leiden University, Isaac Vossius Latin<-
->4. 7.)<-

->Edited by GEORGE HENDERSON, M.A., PH.D.<-

---

->(*Issued 1900. See New Edition.*)<-

3. *The Poems of Egan O’Rahilly* <note>original Irish: "Dánta Aoḋagáin Uí Raṫaille"</note>. Complete Edition.<-

->Edited, chiefly from manuscripts in Maynooth College, by<-
->REV. P. S. DINNEEN, M.A. <note>Dinneen is best known for compiling a major Irish-English dictionary.</note><-

---

->(*Issued 1901.*)<-

4. *The History of Ireland* <note>original Irish: "Foras Feasa ar Éirinn"</note>. By<-
->GEOFFREY KEATING.<-

->Edited by DAVID COMYN, Esq., M.R.I.A. <term>Member of the Royal Irish Academy</term><-
->(Part I formed the Society’s volume for 1901.)<-

<summary>This page catalogs the first four volumes published by the Irish Texts Society, including works by Douglas Hyde and Patrick Dinneen, covering Irish mythology, poetry, and history.</summary>
<keywords>Irish Texts Society, publications, Douglas Hyde, George Henderson, P. S. Dinneen, David Comyn, Geoffrey Keating, Irish manuscripts, Book of the Dun Cow, Egan O’Rahilly</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the catalog of the Irish Texts Society's publications, moving from the early 1900s into the first decade of the 20th century. It lists volumes 5 through 11, detailing significant works of medieval romance, classical translation, and historical poetry.</meta>

->(Issued 1904.)<-

5. *The Martial Career of Conghal Cláiringneach* <note>original: "Caitréim Congail Cláiringniġ"</note>, preserved in a paper manuscript of the seventeenth century in the Royal Irish Academy (manuscript reference 23 H. 1 C.).

->Edited by PATRICK M. MacSWEENEY, M.A.<-

---

->(Issued 1907.)<-

6. *The Irish Version of Virgil's Aeneid* from the *Book of Ballymote* <note>The Book of Ballymote is a major 14th-century Irish manuscript containing historical, genealogical, and literary texts.</note>.

->Edited by REV. GEORGE CALDER, B.D.<-

---

->(Issued 1908.)<-

7. *The Poem-Book of Finn* <note>original: "Duanaire Finn"</note> [Ossianic poems from the library of the Franciscan Monastery, Dublin].

->Edited by Professor JOHN MacNEILL.<-

---

->(Issued 1908.)<-

8 and 9. Volumes II and III of Keating's *History of Ireland*.

->Edited by REV. P. S. DINNEEN, M.A.<-

---

->(Issued 1909.)<-

10. *Two Arthurian Romances* [*The Adventure of the Lad of the Eagle* and *The Adventure of the Crop-Eared Dog*] <note>original: "Eaċtra Macaoiṁ an Iolair agus Eaċtra an Maḋra Maoil"</note>.

->Edited by Professor R. A. S. MACALISTER, M.A.<-

---

->(Issued 1910.)<-

11. *Poems of David O'Bruadair* (Part I).

->Edited (in three volumes) by REV. J. MacERLEAN, S.J. <term>S.J. stands for Society of Jesus, identifying MacErlean as a Jesuit priest.</term><-

<summary>This page lists volumes 5 through 11 of the Irish Texts Society’s publications, featuring edited versions of medieval Irish romances, Virgil's Aeneid, and the works of the poet David O'Bruadair.</summary>
